{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01BBdxzSALFNzvSfGnJPcZGf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_018qJDKGBt2epMezKFGHxdMr","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/first-order-system-identification.md","content":"# First-Order System Identification for Temperature Control\n\n## Overview\nIdentifying the dynamics of a first-order linear system (room temperature response to heater power) is critical for controller tuning. This skill covers step response characterization and parameter estimation using curve fitting.\n\n## System Model\nA first-order system is modeled as:\n```\ndT/dt = (1/tau) * (K * u - T + T_ambient)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- `T` = temperature (°C)\n- `u` = heater power (0-100%)\n- `K` = steady-state gain (°C per 1% power)\n- `tau` = time constant (seconds)\n- `T_ambient` = ambient temperature (°C)\n\nIn steady state: ΔT_ss = K * Δu\n\n## Step Response Data Collection\n1. **Initial condition**: Record initial temperature for 5-10 seconds\n2. **Step input**: Apply constant heater power (e.g., 50%)\n3. **Data sampling**: Collect temperature readings at ~0.5-1.0s intervals for 30+ seconds\n4. **Required**: At least 20 data points to ensure good curve fitting\n\n## Parameter Estimation Using Curve Fitting\nUse exponential fit to a step response:\n```\nT(t) = T_initial + ΔT_ss * (1 - exp(-t/tau))\n```\n\nKey metrics:\n- `K` = steady-state gain = (T_final - T_initial) / heater_power\n- `tau` = time constant (fit the exponential curve)\n- `r_squared` = goodness of fit (target: >0.95)\n\n## Python Implementation\n```python\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef first_order_response(t, K, tau, T_ambient):\n    \"\"\"First-order system response to step input\"\"\"\n    u = 50.0  # step input\n    T_ss = T_ambient + K * u\n    return T_ambient + (T_ss - T_ambient) * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n# Extract data\ntimes = np.array([d['time'] for d in data])\ntemps = np.array([d['temperature'] for d in data])\nT_ambient = temps[0]\n\n# Fit curve\npopt, _ = curve_fit(first_order_response, times, temps,\n                    p0=[0.1, 20.0, T_ambient], maxfev=5000)\nK, tau, _ = popt\n\n# Calculate R²\nresiduals = temps - first_order_response(times, K, tau, T_ambient)\nss_res = np.sum(residuals**2)\nss_tot = np.sum((temps - np.mean(temps))**2)\nr_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- Use heater power between 30-70% to get good dynamics without saturation\n- Ensure at least 30 seconds of data collection\n- Discard initial transients if sensor takes time to stabilize\n- Verify R² > 0.90 for reliable parameter estimation\n- Account for sensor noise (±2°C) in data interpretation\n\n## References\n- Control System Design, Astrom & Murray\n- First-order system step response analysis\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":118,"cache_read_input_tokens":19545,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":118,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a82e4bf3-878a-4855-bd46-df0cad137d16","uuid":"ee450a1c-21a7-489b-bd53-159c110c80ee"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01W8KJzB7Dr6znL6hVxoojE7","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01QyhS8HeXG2xXHXsUpLLMbN","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/pid-controller-tuning.md","content":"# PID Controller Tuning for First-Order Systems\n\n## Overview\nPID (Proportional-Integral-Derivative) controller design for temperature control in first-order systems. This skill covers gain calculation, tuning strategies, and implementation.\n\n## Controller Structure\nA PID controller computes:\n```\nu(t) = Kp * e(t) + Ki * ∫e(t)dt + Kd * de/dt\n```\n\nFor discretized systems (sampling time Ts):\n```\nu[n] = Kp * e[n] + Ki * Ts * Σe[n] + Kd * (e[n] - e[n-1]) / Ts\n```\n\nWhere:\n- `Kp` = proportional gain\n- `Ki` = integral gain\n- `Kd` = derivative gain\n- `e(t)` = error (setpoint - temperature)\n\n## Tuning Method: Lambda Tuning (for First-Order Systems)\n\nFor a first-order system with parameters K and tau, lambda tuning provides:\n\n```\nKp = tau / (K * lambda)\nKi = Kp / tau\nKd = 0  (typically not needed for temperature control)\n```\n\nWhere `lambda` is a design parameter (time constant of closed-loop response):\n- Smaller lambda → faster response, but more aggressive\n- Larger lambda → slower response, more stable\n- Typical range: lambda = 1.5 * tau to 3 * tau\n\n## Performance Trade-offs\n| Lambda Range | Characteristics |\n|---|---|\n| 1-2 * tau | Fast response, potential overshoot |\n| 2-3 * tau | Balanced response, moderate overshoot |\n| 3-5 * tau | Slow response, minimal overshoot |\n\n## Practical Tuning Formula\nFor temperature control with typical requirements:\n1. Estimate system parameters K and tau from calibration\n2. Choose lambda based on overshoot tolerance\n3. Calculate gains:\n   - `Kp = tau / (K * lambda)`\n   - `Ki = Kp / tau`\n   - `Kd = 0`\n\n## Implementation Considerations\n\n### Anti-windup for Integral Term\n```python\n# Clamp integral error accumulation\nintegral_error = min(max(integral_error, -integral_max), integral_max)\n```\n\n### Output Saturation (0-100% heater power)\n```python\ncontrol_output = np.clip(control_output, 0, 100)\n```\n\n### Derivative Filtering\nFor noisy temperature sensors, apply low-pass filtering to derivative:\n```\nKd_filtered = Kd * (N / (1 + N * Ts))  # N is filter coefficient\n```\n\n## Example Implementation\n```python\nclass PIDController:\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, ts=0.5):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.ts = ts\n        self.integral_error = 0\n        self.last_error = 0\n\n    def update(self, setpoint, temperature):\n        error = setpoint - temperature\n\n        # Proportional term\n        p_term = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term\n        self.integral_error += error * self.ts\n        self.integral_error = np.clip(self.integral_error, -100, 100)\n        i_term = self.Ki * self.integral_error\n\n        # Derivative term\n        d_term = self.Kd * (error - self.last_error) / self.ts\n\n        # Output\n        output = p_term + i_term + d_term\n        output = np.clip(output, 0, 100)\n\n        self.last_error = error\n        return output\n```\n\n## Tuning for Target Specifications\nGiven targets:\n- Steady-state error < 0.5°C\n- Settling time < 120s\n- Overshoot < 10%\n- Control duration ≥ 150s\n\nSuggested lambda selection:\n- Start with lambda = 2.5 * tau\n- If overshoot > 10%, increase lambda\n- If settling time > 120s, decrease lambda\n\n## References\n- Control Systems Engineering, Norman Nise\n- Lambda Tuning Theory, Gerry Sheridan\n- PID Controller Tuning Guide\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":914,"cache_read_input_tokens":19663,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":914,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a82e4bf3-878a-4855-bd46-df0cad137d16","uuid":"2607f1be-a82f-40b3-b96b-48e0498de77e"}

This skill covers gain calculation, tuning strategies, and implementation.\n\n## Controller Structure\nA PID controller computes:\n```\nu(t) = Kp * e(t) + Ki * ∫e(t)dt + Kd * de/dt\n```\n\nFor discretized systems (sampling time Ts):\n```\nu[n] = Kp * e[n] + Ki * Ts * Σe[n] + Kd * (e[n] - e[n-1]) / Ts\n```\n\nWhere:\n- `Kp` = proportional gain\n- `Ki` = integral gain\n- `Kd` = derivative gain\n- `e(t)` = error (setpoint - temperature)\n\n## Tuning Method: Lambda Tuning (for First-Order Systems)\n\nFor a first-order system with parameters K and tau, lambda tuning provides:\n\n```\nKp = tau / (K * lambda)\nKi = Kp / tau\nKd = 0  (typically not needed for temperature control)\n```\n\nWhere `lambda` is a design parameter (time constant of closed-loop response):\n- Smaller lambda → faster response, but more aggressive\n- Larger lambda → slower response, more stable\n- Typical range: lambda = 1.5 * tau to 3 * tau\n\n## Performance Trade-offs\n| Lambda Range | Characteristics |\n|---|---|\n| 1-2 * tau | Fast response, potential overshoot |\n| 2-3 * tau | Balanced response, moderate overshoot |\n| 3-5 * tau | Slow response, minimal overshoot |\n\n## Practical Tuning Formula\nFor temperature control with typical requirements:\n1. Estimate system parameters K and tau from calibration\n2. Choose lambda based on overshoot tolerance\n3. Calculate gains:\n   - `Kp = tau / (K * lambda)`\n   - `Ki = Kp / tau`\n   - `Kd = 0`\n\n## Implementation Considerations\n\n### Anti-windup for Integral Term\n```python\n# Clamp integral error accumulation\nintegral_error = min(max(integral_error, -integral_max), integral_max)\n```\n\n### Output Saturation (0-100% heater power)\n```python\ncontrol_output = np.clip(control_output, 0, 100)\n```\n\n### Derivative Filtering\nFor noisy temperature sensors, apply low-pass filtering to derivative:\n```\nKd_filtered = Kd * (N / (1 + N * Ts))  # N is filter coefficient\n```\n\n## Example Implementation\n```python\nclass PIDController:\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, ts=0.5):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.ts = ts\n        self.integral_error = 0\n        self.last_error = 0\n\n    def update(self, setpoint, temperature):\n        error = setpoint - temperature\n\n        # Proportional term\n        p_term = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term\n        self.integral_error += error * self.ts\n        self.integral_error = np.clip(self.integral_error, -100, 100)\n        i_term = self.Ki * self.integral_error\n\n        # Derivative term\n        d_term = self.Kd * (error - self.last_error) / self.ts\n\n        # Output\n        output = p_term + i_term + d_term\n        output = np.clip(output, 0, 100)\n\n        self.last_error = error\n        return output\n```\n\n## Tuning for Target Specifications\nGiven targets:\n- Steady-state error < 0.5°C\n- Settling time < 120s\n- Overshoot < 10%\n- Control duration ≥ 150s\n\nSuggested lambda selection:\n- Start with lambda = 2.5 * tau\n- If overshoot > 10%, increase lambda\n- If settling time > 120s, decrease lambda\n\n## References\n- Control Systems Engineering, Norman Nise\n- Lambda Tuning Theory, Gerry Sheridan\n- PID Controller Tuning Guide\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Python Data Logging and JSON Management\n\n## Overview\nEfficient data collection, storage, and analysis for control system experiments using JSON format for structured data storage and retrieval.\n\n## JSON Format for Control Experiments\n\n### Calibration Log Structure\n```json\n{\n  \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n  \"heater_power_test\": 50.0,\n  \"initial_temperature\": 18.0,\n  \"duration_seconds\": 30,\n  \"data\": [\n    {\"time\": 0.0, \"temperature\": 18.0, \"heater_power\": 0.0},\n    {\"time\": 0.5, \"temperature\": 18.05, \"heater_power\": 50.0}\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n### Control Log Structure\n```json\n{\n  \"phase\": \"control\",\n  \"setpoint\": 22.0,\n  \"controller_type\": \"PID\",\n  \"start_time\": \"2025-01-29T10:30:00\",\n  \"duration_seconds\": 150,\n  \"data\": [\n    {\n      \"time\": 0.0,\n      \"temperature\": 18.0,\n      \"setpoint\": 22.0,\n      \"heater_power\": 0.0,\n      \"error\": 4.0,\n      \"integral_error\": 0.0,\n      \"derivative_error\": 0.0\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n## Python Implementation\n\n### Data Collection Class\n```python\nimport json\nfrom datetime import datetime\n\nclass DataLogger:\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.data = []\n        self.phase = None\n        self.metadata = {}\n\n    def add_point(self, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"Add a data point with timestamp\"\"\"\n        point = {\"time\": kwargs.pop(\"time\", None)}\n        point.update(kwargs)\n        self.data.append(point)\n\n    def save_to_json(self, filepath):\n        \"\"\"Save logged data to JSON file\"\"\"\n        output = {\n            \"phase\": self.phase,\n            **self.metadata,\n            \"data\": self.data\n        }\n        with open(filepath, 'w') as f:\n            json.dump(output, f, indent=2)\n        return filepath\n\n    def load_from_json(self, filepath):\n        \"\"\"Load data from JSON file\"\"\"\n        with open(filepath, 'r') as f:\n            data = json.load(f)\n        self.phase = data.pop(\"phase\", None)\n        self.metadata = {k: v for k, v in data.items() if k != \"data\"}\n        self.data = data.get(\"data\", [])\n        return self\n```\n\n### Time Management\n```python\nimport time\n\nclass Timer:\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.start_time = time.time()\n        self.last_time = self.start_time\n\n    def elapsed(self):\n        \"\"\"Return elapsed time since start\"\"\"\n        return time.time() - self.start_time\n\n    def sleep_until(self, target_time):\n        \"\"\"Sleep until target elapsed time\"\"\"\n        elapsed = self.elapsed()\n        if elapsed < target_time:\n            time.sleep(target_time - elapsed)\n\n    def reset(self):\n        \"\"\"Reset timer\"\"\"\n        self.start_time = time.time()\n```\n\n### Metrics Calculation\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef calculate_metrics(data, setpoint, settling_band=0.5):\n    \"\"\"Calculate control performance metrics\"\"\"\n retrieval.\n\n## JSON Format for Control Experiments\n\n### Calibration Log Structure\n```json\n{\n  \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n  \"heater_power_test\": 50.0,\n  \"initial_temperature\": 18.0,\n  \"duration_seconds\": 30,\n  \"data\": [\n    {\"time\": 0.0, \"temperature\": 18.0, \"heater_power\": 0.0},\n    {\"time\": 0.5, \"temperature\": 18.05, \"heater_power\": 50.0}\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n### Control Log Structure\n```json\n{\n  \"phase\": \"control\",\n  \"setpoint\": 22.0,\n  \"controller_type\": \"PID\",\n  \"start_time\": \"2025-01-29T10:30:00\",\n  \"duration_seconds\": 150,\n  \"data\": [\n    {\n      \"time\": 0.0,\n      \"temperature\": 18.0,\n      \"setpoint\": 22.0,\n      \"heater_power\": 0.0,\n      \"error\": 4.0,\n      \"integral_error\": 0.0,\n      \"derivative_error\": 0.0\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n## Python Implementation\n\n### Data Collection Class\n```python\nimport json\nfrom datetime import datetime\n\nclass DataLogger:\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.data = []\n        self.phase = None\n        self.metadata = {}\n\n    def add_point(self, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"Add a data point with timestamp\"\"\"\n        point = {\"time\": kwargs.pop(\"time\", None)}\n        point.update(kwargs)\n        self.data.append(point)\n\n    def save_to_json(self, filepath):\n        \"\"\"Save logged data to JSON file\"\"\"\n        output = {\n            \"phase\": self.phase,\n            **self.metadata,\n            \"data\": self.data\n        }\n        with open(filepath, 'w') as f:\n            json.dump(output, f, indent=2)\n        return filepath\n\n    def load_from_json(self, filepath):\n        \"\"\"Load data from JSON file\"\"\"\n        with open(filepath, 'r') as f:\n            data = json.load(f)\n        self.phase = data.pop(\"phase\", None)\n        self.metadata = {k: v for k, v in data.items() if k != \"data\"}\n        self.data = data.get(\"data\", [])\n        return self\n```\n\n### Time \"\"\"Return elapsed time since start\"\"\"\n        return time.time() - self.start_time\n\n    def sleep_until(self, target_time):\n        \"\"\"Sleep until target elapsed time\"\"\"\n This module simulates a room with heating capabilities.\n\n## Simulator API\n\n### Initialization\n```python\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\nsimulator = HVACSimulator()\n```\n\n### Key Methods\n\n#### `get_temperature()`\nReturns current room temperature in °C\n```python\ntemp = simulator.get_temperature()  # Returns float, e.g., 18.0\n```\n\nCharacteristics:\n- Returns initial temperature ~18.0°C with ±2°C sensor noise\n- Updates based on previous heater power setting\n- Includes realistic thermal dynamics\n\n#### `set_heater_power(power)`\nSets heater power level (0-100%)\n```python\nsimulator.set_heater_power(50.0)  # Set to 50% power\nsimulator.set_heater_power(0)     # Turn off heater\n```\n\nConstraints:\n- Valid range: 0.0 to 100.0\n- Must be set before calling get_temperature() to take effect\n- Changes apply with one-step delay in first-order system\n\n### Simulator Characteristics\n- **Time step**: Typically 0.5 seconds between calls\n- **Dynamics**: First-order system behavior\n- **Sensor noise**: ±2°C random variation\n- **Ambient temperature**: Room loss to ambient ~18°C baseline\n- **Maximum power effect**: ~1°C rise per 10% power per 10 seconds\n\n## Typical Usage Pattern\n\n```python\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\nimport time\n\nsimulator = HVACSimulator()\n\n# Initial measurement\ntemp = simulator.get_temperature()\nprint(f\"Initial temperature: {temp}°C\")\n\n# Control loop\nfor iteration in range(100):\n    # Measure\n    current_temp = simulator.get_temperature()\n\n    # Control action (e.g., PID)\n    error = setpoint - current_temp\n    power = calculate_control_action(error)\n\n    # Apply\n    simulator.set_heater_power(power)\n\n    # Wait for thermal response\n    time.sleep(0.5)\n```\n\n## Simulator Behavior Assumptions\n\n### Step Response Example\nStarting at 18°C with 50% constant heater power:\n- 0s: T ≈ 18.0°C\n- 5s: T ≈ 18.5°C (rising slowly)\n- 10s: T ≈ 19.0°C\n- 20s: T ≈ 20.2°C (asymptotic approach)\n- 30s: T ≈ 21.0°C\n- 60s: T ≈ 23.5°C (steady state)\n\nSteady-state gain K ≈ 0.1°C per 1% power\nTime constant tau ≈ 20-30 seconds\n\n### Sensor Noise\n- Random ±2°C noise on each reading\n- Decorrelated between samples\n- Use smoothing/filtering for noisy measurements\n\n## Calibration Recommendations\n\nTo characterize your specific simulator instance:\n1. Record initial temperature (5-10 seconds at 0% power)\n2. Apply constant heater power (50%) for 30+ seconds\n3. Fit first-order model to step response\n4. Extract K and tau parameters\n5. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_019UEGM7XVJuUbvGSXRbnWWa","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_016Ec41XmJwrozrDScYiYxBW","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/control-system-metrics.md","content":"# Control System Performance Metrics\n\n## Overview\nStandard metrics for evaluating closed-loop control system performance, particularly for temperature regulation. These metrics quantify how well the controller meets design requirements.\n\n## Key Metrics\n\n### 1. Rise Time\n**Definition**: Time for response to rise from 10% to 90% of final value\n```\nrise_time = t(90%) - t(10%)\n```\n\n**Interpretation**:\n- Fast response: rise_time < 30s\n- Moderate: 30-60s\n- Slow: > 60s\n\n**Calculation**:\n```python\ndef calc_rise_time(times, temps, setpoint):\n    t10_idx = np.where(temps >= setpoint * 0.1)[0]\n    t90_idx = np.where(temps >= setpoint * 0.9)[0]\n    if len(t10_idx) > 0 and len(t90_idx) > 0:\n        return times[t90_idx[0]] - times[t10_idx[0]]\n    return None\n```\n\n### 2. Overshoot\n**Definition**: Maximum temperature exceeds setpoint\n```\novershoot (%) = (T_max - setpoint) / setpoint × 100\n```\n\n**Targets**:\n- Excellent: < 5%\n- Good: < 10%\n- Acceptable: < 20%\n\n**Calculation**:\n```python\ndef calc_overshoot(temps, setpoint):\n    T_max = np.max(temps)\n    return ((T_max - setpoint) / setpoint) * 100\n```\n\n### 3. Settling Time\n**Definition**: Time for response to enter and remain within settling band (±tolerance)\n```\nsettling_time = first time when |T - setpoint| ≤ tolerance for remainder of test\n```\n\n**Standard band**: ±0.5°C to ±2% of setpoint\n\n**Targets**:\n- Fast: < 60s\n- Moderate: 60-120s\n- Slow: > 120s\n\n**Calculation**:\n```python\ndef calc_settling_time(times, temps, setpoint, band=0.5):\n    settled = np.abs(temps - setpoint) <= band\n    # Check last portion for stability\n    if np.all(settled[-50:]):  # All last 50 points in band\n        idx = np.where(settled)[0][0]\n        return times[idx]\n    return None\n```\n\n### 4. Steady-State Error\n**Definition**: Average error in final steady state\n```\nSSE = mean(|T_final - setpoint|) over last period\n```\n\n**Targets**:\n- Excellent: < 0.2°C\n- Good: < 0.5°C\n- Acceptable: < 1.0°C\n\n**Calculation**:\n```python\ndef calc_steady_state_error(temps, setpoint, window=50):\n    final_temps = temps[-window:]\n    return np.mean(np.abs(final_temps - setpoint))\n```\n\n### 5. Maximum Temperature\n**Definition**: Peak temperature reached during control\n```\nT_max = max(T(t)) for all t\n```\n\n**Constraints**: Must not exceed safety limits (e.g., < 30°C for HVAC)\n\n**Calculation**:\n```python\ndef calc_max_temp(temps):\n    return np.max(temps)\n```\n\n## Comprehensive Metrics Dictionary\n\n```python\ndef calculate_all_metrics(data, setpoint, safety_limit=30.0):\n    \"\"\"Calculate all performance metrics from control log\"\"\"\n    times = np.array([d['time'] for d in data])\n    temps = np.array([d['temperature'] for d in data])\n\n    metrics = {\n        \"rise_time\": calc_rise_time(times, temps, setpoint),\n        \"overshoot\": calc_overshoot(temps, setpoint),\n        \"settling_time\": calc_settling_time(times, temps, setpoint),\n        \"steady_state_error\": calc_steady_state_error(temps, setpoint),\n        \"max_temp\": float(np.max(temps)),\n        \"meets_specs\": {\n            \"steady_state_error\": calc_steady_state_error(temps, setpoint) < 0.5,\n            \"settling_time\": calc_settling_time(times, temps, setpoint) is not None,\n            \"overshoot\": calc_overshoot(temps, setpoint) < 10.0,\n            \"max_temp\": np.max(temps) < safety_limit\n        }\n    }\n    return metrics\n```\n\n## Test Duration Considerations\n\nFor reliable metrics:\n- **Minimum**: 120 seconds (to measure settling)\n- **Recommended**: 150-180 seconds (to observe steady-state behavior)\n- **Overshoot duration**: Must capture peak before settling\n- **Steady-state window**: Use final 50+ data points\n\n## Visualization Tips\n\n```python\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\ndef plot_control_response(times, temps, setpoint, metrics):\n    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))\n    plt.plot(times, temps, 'b-', label='Temperature')\n    plt.axhline(setpoint, 'r--', label='Setpoint')\n    plt.axhline(setpoint - 0.5, 'g--', alpha=0.5, label='±0.5°C Band')\n    plt.axhline(setpoint + 0.5, 'g--', alpha=0.5)\n\n    if metrics['overshoot'] is not None:\n        plt.text(0.5, 0.95, f\"Overshoot: {metrics['overshoot']:.1f}%\",\n                 transform=plt.gca().transAxes)\n    if metrics['settling_time'] is not None:\n        plt.axvline(metrics['settling_time'], 'orange', linestyle=':',\n                    label=f\"Settling: {metrics['settling_time']:.0f}s\")\n\n    plt.xlabel('Time (s)')\n    plt.ylabel('Temperature (°C)')\n    plt.legend()\n    plt.grid(True, alpha=0.3)\n    plt.show()\n```\n\n## References\n- Control System Design, Astrom & Murray\n- Process Control Module, ISA (Instrument Society of America)\n- Standard metrics for closed-loop systems\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1022,"cache_read_input_tokens":23235,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1022,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a82e4bf3-878a-4855-bd46-df0cad137d16","uuid":"f103957a-0b48-4d05-bfdb-c75fb2d8468f"}

Settling Time\n**Definition**: Time for response to enter and remain within settling band (±tolerance)\n```\nsettling_time = first time when |T - setpoint| ≤ tolerance for remainder of test\n```\n\n**Standard band**: ±0.5°C to ±2% of setpoint\n\n**Targets**:\n- Fast: < 60s\n- Moderate: 60-120s\n- Slow: > 120s\n\n**Calculation**:\n```python\ndef calc_settling_time(times, temps, setpoint, band=0.5):\n    settled = np.abs(temps - setpoint) <= band\n    # Check last portion for stability\n    if np.all(settled[-50:]):  # All last 50 points in band\n        idx = np.where(settled)[0][0]\n        return times[idx]\n    return None\n```\n\n### 4. Steady-State Error\n**Definition**: Average error in final steady state\n```\nSSE = mean(|T_final - setpoint|) over last period\n```\n\n**Targets**:\n- Excellent: < 0.2°C\n- Good: < 0.5°C\n- Acceptable: < 1.0°C\n\n**Calculation**:\n```python\ndef calc_steady_state_error(temps, setpoint, window=50):\n    final_temps = temps[-window:]\n    return np.mean(np.abs(final_temps - setpoint))\n```\n\n### 5. Maximum Temperature\n**Definition**: Peak temperature reached during control\n```\nT_max = max(T(t)) for all t\n```\n\n**Constraints**: Must not exceed safety limits (e.g., < 30°C for HVAC)\n\n**Calculation**:\n```python\ndef calc_max_temp(temps):\n    return np.max(temps)\n```\n\n## Comprehensive Metrics Dictionary\n\n```python\ndef calculate_all_metrics(data, setpoint, safety_limit=30.0):\n    \"\"\"Calculate all performance metrics from control log\"\"\"\n    times = np.array([d['time'] for d in data])\n    temps = np.array([d['temperature'] for d in data])\n\n    metrics = {\n        \"rise_time\": calc_rise_time(times, temps, setpoint),\n        \"overshoot\": calc_overshoot(temps, setpoint),\n        \"settling_time\": calc_settling_time(times, temps, setpoint),\n        \"steady_state_error\": calc_steady_state_error(temps, setpoint),\n        \"max_temp\": float(np.max(temps)),\n        \"meets_specs\": {\n            \"steady_state_error\": calc_steady_state_error(temps, setpoint) < 0.5,\n            \"settling_time\": calc_settling_time(times, temps, setpoint) is not None,\n            \"overshoot\": calc_overshoot(temps, setpoint) < 10.0,\n            \"max_temp\": np.max(temps) < safety_limit\n        }\n    }\n    return metrics\n```\n\n## Test Duration Considerations\n\nFor reliable metrics:\n- **Minimum**: 120 seconds (to measure settling)\n- **Recommended**: 150-180 seconds (to observe steady-state behavior)\n- **Overshoot duration**: Must capture peak before settling\n- **Steady-state window**: Use final 50+ data points\n\n## Visualization Tips\n\n```python\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\ndef plot_control_response(times, temps, setpoint, metrics):\n    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))\n    plt.plot(times, temps, 'b-', label='Temperature')\n    plt.axhline(setpoint, 'r--', label='Setpoint')\n    plt.axhline(setpoint - 0.5, 'g--', alpha=0.5, label='±0.5°C Band')\n    plt.axhline(setpoint + 0.5, 'g--', alpha=0.5)\n\n    if metrics['overshoot'] is not None:\n        plt.text(0.5, 0.95, f\"Overshoot: {metrics['overshoot']:.1f}%\",\n                 transform=plt.gca().transAxes)\n    if metrics['settling_time'] is not None:\n        plt.axvline(metrics['settling_time'], 'orange', linestyle=':',\n                    label=f\"Settling: {metrics['settling_time']:.0f}s\")\n\n    plt.xlabel('Time (s)')\n    plt.ylabel('Temperature (°C)')\n    plt.legend()\n    plt.grid(True, alpha=0.3)\n    plt.show()\n```\n\n## References\n- Control System Design, Astrom & Murray\n- Process Control Module, ISA (Instrument Society of America)\n- Standard metrics for closed-loop systems\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Calibration: Characterize room thermal dynamics\n2. Parameter estimation: Extract K and tau\n3. PID tuning: Calculate controller gains\n4. Closed-loop control: Maintain setpoint temperature\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\n\nclass TemperatureController:\n    \"\"\"PID controller for temperature regulation\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, ts=0.5):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.ts = ts\n        self.integral_error = 0\n        self.last_error = 0\n\n    def update(self, setpoint, temperature):\n        \"\"\"Calculate control output using PID\"\"\"\n        error = setpoint - temperature\n\n        # Proportional term\n        p_term = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term with anti-windup\n        self.integral_error += error * self.ts\n        self.integral_error = np.clip(self.integral_error, -100, 100)\n        i_term = self.Ki * self.integral_error\n\n        # Derivative term\n        d_term = self.Kd * (error - self.last_error) / self.ts\n\n        # Calculate output\n        output = p_term + i_term + d_term\n        output = np.clip(output, 0, 100)\n\n        self.last_error = error\n        return output\n\n\nclass ControlSystemExperiment:\n    \"\"\"Main experiment orchestration\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.simulator = HVACSimulator()\n        self.dt = self.simulator.get_dt()\n        self.setpoint = self.simulator.get_setpoint()\n\n    def run_calibration(self, heater_power=50.0, duration=30.0):\n        \"\"\"Run calibration test with constant heater power\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== CALIBRATION TEST ===\")\n        print(f\"Duration: {duration}s, Heater Power: {heater_power}%\")\n\n        # Format data for output\n        data = []\n        for i, result in enumerate(results):\n            data.append({\n                \"time\": result[\"time\"],\n                \"temperature\": result[\"temperature\"],\n                \"heater_power\": result[\"heater_power\"]\n            })\n\n        # Save calibration log\n        calibration_log = {\n            \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n            \"heater_power_test\": heater_power,\n            \"initial_temperature\": round(initial_temp, 2),\n            \"duration_seconds\": duration,\n            \"data\": data\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/calibration_log.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(calibration_log, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Collected {len(data)} data points\")\n        print(f\"Final temperature: {data[-1]['temperature']:.2f}°C\")\n        print(f\"Calibration log saved to: /root/calibration_log.json\")\n\n        return calibration_log\n\n    def estimate_parameters(self, calibration_log):\n        \"\"\"Estimate system parameters K and tau from calibration data\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PARAMETER ESTIMATION ===\")\n\n        data = calibration_log[\"data\"]\n        times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n        temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n        heater_power = calibration_log[\"heater_power_test\"]\n\n        # Initial temperature and ambient\n        T_initial = temps[0]\n        T_ambient = self.simulator.get_ambient_temp()\n\n        # Define first-order response model\n        def first_order_response(t, K, tau):\n            T_ss = T_ambient + K * heater_power\n            return T_ambient + (T_ss - T_ambient) * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n        try:\n            # Fit curve to data\n            popt, pcov = curve_fit(\n                first_order_response,\n                times,\n                temps,\n                p0=[0.1, 20.0],\n                maxfev=10000\n            )\n            K, tau = popt\n\n            # Calculate R² goodness of fit\n            predicted = first_order_response(times, K, tau)\n            residuals = temps - predicted\n            ss_res = np.sum(residuals**2)\n            ss_tot = np.sum((temps - np.mean(temps))**2)\n            r_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\n            fitting_error = np.sqrt(np.mean(residuals**2))\n\n            print(f\"Estimated K (gain): {K:.4f} °C per 1% power\")\n            print(f\"Estimated tau (time constant): {tau:.2f} seconds\")\n            print(f\"R² (goodness of fit): {r_squared:.4f}\")\n            print(f\"RMS fitting error: {fitting_error:.4f}°C\")\n\n            # Save estimated parameters\n            params = {\n                \"K\": round(K, 4),\n                \"tau\": round(tau, 2),\n                \"r_squared\": round(r_squared, 4),\n                \"fitting_error\": round(fitting_error, 4)\n            }\n\n            with open(\"/root/estimated_params.json\", \"w\") as f:\n                json.dump(params, f, indent=2)\n\n            print(f\"Parameters saved to: /root/estimated_params.json\")\n\n            return params\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error during parameter estimation: {e}\")\n            raise\n\n    def tune_pid_gains(self, params):\n        \"\"\"Calculate PID gains using lambda tuning method\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PID GAIN TUNING ===\")\n\n        K = params[\"K\"]\n        tau = params[\"tau\"]\n\n        # Lambda tuning: choose lambda for desired closed-loop response\n        # For this application: balance between speed and overshoot\n        lambda_tc = 2.5 * tau  # Choose lambda = 2.5 * tau\n\n        # Lambda tuning formulas for first-order system\n        Kp = tau / (K * lambda_tc)\n        Ki = Kp / tau\n        Kd = 0.0  # Not needed for temperature control\n\n        print(f\"Lambda (desired closed-loop time constant): {lambda_tc:.2f}s\")\n        print(f\"Kp (proportional gain): {Kp:.4f}\")\n        print(f\"Ki (integral gain): {Ki:.6f}\")\n        print(f\"Kd (derivative gain): {Kd:.4f}\")\n\n        # Reset simulator\n        initial_temp = self.simulator.reset()\n\n        # Control loop\n        data = []\n        start_time = time.time()\n\n        # Run for duration seconds\n        step_count = int(duration / self.dt)\n\n        for step in range(step_count):\n            # Get current temperature measurement\n            result = self.simulator.step(0.0)  # Get measurement at current power\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            elapsed_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n            # Calculate control action\n            control_output = controller.update(self.setpoint, current_temp)\n\n            # Apply control\n            result = self.simulator.step(control_output)\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            elapsed_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n            # Log data\n            error = self.setpoint - current_temp\n            data.append({\n                \"time\": round(elapsed_time, 2),\n                \"temperature\": round(current_temp, 2),\n                \"setpoint\": round(self.setpoint, 2),\n                \"heater_power\": round(control_output, 2),\n                \"error\": round(error, 2)\n            })\n\n            # Print progress\n            if step % 20 == 0:\n                print(f\"t={elapsed_time:.1f}s: T={current_temp:.2f}°C, error={error:.2f}°C, power={control_output:.0f}%\")\n\n            # Check safety limit\n            if result[\"safety_triggered\"]:\n                print(f\"Safety limit triggered at t={elapsed_time:.1f}s\")\n                break\n\n        print(f\"\\nControl completed: {len(data)} data points collected\")\n        print(f\"Final temperature: {data[-1]['temperature']:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Overshoot\n        max_temp = np.max(temps)\n        overshoot = ((max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint * 100) if setpoint > 0 else 0\n\n        # Settling time (time to enter ±0.5°C band and stay)\n        settling_band = 0.5\n        in_band = np.abs(temps - setpoint) <= settling_band\n        if np.all(in_band[-50:]):  # Check last 50 points\n            idx_settle = np.where(in_band)[0]\n            if len(idx_settle) > 0:\n                settling_time = times[idx_settle[0]]\n            else:\n                settling_time = None\n        else:\n            settling_time = None\n\n        # Steady-state error\n        final_temps = temps[-50:]\n        steady_state_error = np.mean(np.abs(final_temps - setpoint))\n\n        # Maximum temperature\n        max_measured_temp = float(np.max(temps))\n\n        # Print metrics\n        print(f\"Rise time (10%-90%): {rise_time:.1f}s\" if rise_time else \"Rise time: N/A\")\n        print(f\"Overshoot: {overshoot:.2f}%\")\n        print(f\"Settling time (±0.5°C): {settling_time:.1f}s\" if settling_time else \"Settling time: N/A (not settled)\")\n        print(f\"Steady-state error: {steady_state_error:.2f}°C\")\n        print(f\"Maximum temperature: {max_measured_temp:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Save metrics\n        metrics = {\n            \"rise_time\": rise_time,\n            \"overshoot\": round(overshoot, 2),\n            \"settling_time\": settling_time,\n            \"steady_state_error\": round(steady_state_error, 2),\n            \"max_temp\": round(max_measured_temp, 2)\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/metrics.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"\\nMetrics saved to: /root/metrics.json\")\n\n        return metrics\n\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Run the complete control experiment\"\"\"\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n    print(\"TEMPERATURE CONTROLLER IMPLEMENTATION\")\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n\n    experiment = ControlSystemExperiment()\n\n    # Save estimated parameters\n            params = {\n                \"K\": round(K, 4),\n                \"tau\": round(tau, 2),\n                \"r_squared\": round(r_squared, 4),\n                \"fitting_error\": round(fitting_error, 4)\n            }\n\n            with open(\"/root/estimated_params.json\", \"w\") as f:\n                json.dump(params, f, indent=2)\n\n            print(f\"Parameters saved to: /root/estimated_params.json\")\n\n            return params\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error during parameter estimation: {e}\")\n            raise\n\n    def tune_pid_gains(self, params):\n        \"\"\"Calculate PID gains using lambda tuning method\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PID GAIN TUNING ===\")\n\n        K = params[\"K\"]\n        tau = params[\"tau\"]\n\n        # Lambda tuning: choose lambda for desired closed-loop response\n        # For this application: balance between speed and overshoot\n        lambda_tc = 2.5 * tau  # Choose lambda = 2.5 * tau\n\n        # Lambda tuning formulas for first-order system\n        Kp = tau / (K * lambda_tc)\n        Ki = Kp / tau\n        Kd = 0.0  # Not needed for temperature control\n\n        print(f\"Lambda (desired closed-loop time constant): {lambda_tc:.2f}s\")\n        print(f\"Kp (proportional gain): {Kp:.4f}\")\n        print(f\"Ki (integral gain): {Ki:.6f}\")\n        print(f\"Kd (derivative gain): {Kd:.4f}\")\n\n        # Save tuned gains\n        gains = {\n            \"Kp\": round(Kp, 4),\n            \"Ki\": round(Ki, 6),\n            \"Kd\": round(Kd, 4),\n            \"lambda\": round(lambda_tc, 2)\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/tuned_gains.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(gains, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Gains saved to: /root/tuned_gains.json\")\n\n        return gains\n\n    def run_closed_loop_control(self, gains, duration=150.0):\n        \"\"\"Run closed-loop control to reach setpoint\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== CLOSED-LOOP CONTROL ===\")\n        print(f\"Target setpoint: {self.setpoint}°C\")\n        print(f\"Control duration: {duration}s\")\n\n        # Create controller\n        controller = TemperatureController(\n            Kp=gains[\"Kp\"],\n            Ki=gains[\"Ki\"],\n            Kd=gains[\"Kd\"],\n            ts=self.dt\n        )\n\n        # Reset simulator\n        initial_temp = self.simulator.reset()\n\n        # Control loop\n        data = []\n        start_time = time.time()\n\n        # Run for duration seconds\n        step_count = int(duration / self.dt)\n\n        for step in range(step_count):\n            # Get current temperature measurement\n            result = self.simulator.step(0.0)  # Get measurement at current power\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            elapsed_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n            # Calculate control action\n            control_output = controller.update(self.setpoint, current_temp)\n\n            # Apply control\n            result = self.simulator.step(control_output)\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            elapsed_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n            # Log data\n            error = self.setpoint - current_temp\n            data.append({\n                \"time\": round(elapsed_time, 2),\n                \"temperature\": round(current_temp, 2),\n                \"setpoint\": round(self.setpoint, 2),\n                \"heater_power\": round(control_output, 2),\n                \"error\": round(error, 2)\n            })\n\n            # Print progress\n            if step % 20 == 0:\n                print(f\"t={elapsed_time:.1f}s: T={current_temp:.2f}°C, error={error:.2f}°C, power={control_output:.0f}%\")\n\n            # Check safety limit\n            if result[\"safety_triggered\"]:\n                print(f\"Safety limit triggered at t={elapsed_time:.1f}s\")\n                break\n\n        print(f\"\\nControl completed: {len(data)} data points collected\")\n        print(f\"Final temperature: {data[-1]['temperature']:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Save control log\n        control_log = {\n            \"phase\": \"control\",\n            \"setpoint\": self.setpoint,\n            \"controller_type\": \"PID\",\n            \"duration_seconds\": duration,\n            \"data\": data\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/control_log.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(control_log, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Control log saved to: /root/control_log.json\")\n\n        return control_log\n\n    def calculate_metrics(self, control_log):\n        \"\"\"Calculate performance metrics from control data\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PERFORMANCE METRICS ===\")\n\n        data = control_log[\"data\"]\n        times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n        temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n        setpoint = control_log[\"setpoint\"]\n\n        # Rise time (10%-90%)\n        idx_10 = np.where(temps >= setpoint * 0.1)[0]\n        idx_90 = np.where(temps >= setpoint * 0.9)[0]\n        if len(idx_10) > 0 and len(idx_90) > 0:\n            rise_time = times[idx_90[0]] - times[idx_10[0]]\n        else:\n            rise_time = None\n\n        # Overshoot\n        max_temp = np.max(temps)\n        overshoot = ((max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint * 100) if setpoint > 0 else 0\n\n        # Settling time (time to enter ±0.5°C band and stay)\n        settling_band = 0.5\n        in_band = np.abs(temps - setpoint) <= settling_band\n        if np.all(in_band[-50:]):  # Check last 50 points\n            idx_settle = np.where(in_band)[0]\n            if len(idx_settle) > 0:\n                settling_time = times[idx_settle[0]]\n            else:\n                settling_time = None\n        else:\n            settling_time = None\n\n        # Steady-state error\n        final_temps = temps[-50:]\n        steady_state_error = np.mean(np.abs(final_temps - setpoint))\n\n        # Maximum temperature\n        max_measured_temp = float(np.max(temps))\n\n        # Print metrics\n        print(f\"Rise time (10%-90%): {rise_time:.1f}s\" if rise_time else \"Rise time: N/A\")\n        print(f\"Overshoot: {overshoot:.2f}%\")\n        print(f\"Settling time (±0.5°C): {settling_time:.1f}s\" if settling_time else \"Settling time: N/A (not settled)\")\n        print(f\"Steady-state error: {steady_state_error:.2f}°C\")\n        print(f\"Maximum temperature: {max_measured_temp:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Check specs\n        specs_met = {\n            \"steady_state_error\": steady_state_error < 0.5,\n            \"settling_time\": settling_time is not None and settling_time < 120,\n            \"overshoot\": overshoot < 10.0,\n            \"max_temp\": max_measured_temp < 30.0\n        }\n\n        print(f\"\\nSpecifications met:\")\n        print(f\"  Steady-state error < 0.5°C: {specs_met['steady_state_error']}\")\n        print(f\"  Settling time < 120s: {specs_met['settling_time']}\")\n        print(f\"  Overshoot < 10%: {specs_met['overshoot']}\")\n        print(f\"  Max temp < 30°C: {specs_met['max_temp']}\")\n\n        # Save metrics\n        metrics = {\n            \"rise_time\": rise_time,\n            \"overshoot\": round(overshoot, 2),\n            \"settling_time\": settling_time,\n            \"steady_state_error\": round(steady_state_error, 2),\n            \"max_temp\": round(max_measured_temp, 2)\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/metrics.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"\\nMetrics saved to: /root/metrics.json\")\n\n        return metrics\n\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Run the complete control experiment\"\"\"\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n    print(\"TEMPERATURE CONTROLLER IMPLEMENTATION\")\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n\n    experiment = ControlSystemExperiment()\n\n    # Calibration: Characterize room thermal dynamics\n2. Parameter estimation: Extract K and tau\n3. PID tuning: Calculate controller gains\n4. Closed-loop control: Maintain setpoint temperature\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\n\nclass TemperatureController:\n    \"\"\"PID controller for temperature regulation\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, ts=0.5):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.ts = ts\n        self.integral_error = 0\n        self.last_error = 0\n\n    def update(self, setpoint, temperature):\n        \"\"\"Calculate control output using PID\"\"\"\n        error = setpoint - temperature\n\n        # Proportional term\n        p_term = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term with anti-windup\n        self.integral_error += error * self.ts\n        self.integral_error = np.clip(self.integral_error, -100, 100)\n        i_term = self.Ki * self.integral_error\n\n        # Derivative term\n        d_term = self.Kd * (error - self.last_error) / self.ts\n\n        # Calculate output\n        output = p_term + i_term + d_term\n        output = np.clip(output, 0, 100)\n\n        self.last_error = error\n        return output\n\n\nclass ControlSystemExperiment:\n    \"\"\"Main experiment orchestration\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.simulator = HVACSimulator()\n        self.dt = self.simulator.get_dt()\n        self.setpoint = self.simulator.get_setpoint()\n\n    def run_calibration(self, heater_power=50.0, duration=30.0):\n        \"\"\"Run calibration test with constant heater power\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== CALIBRATION TEST ===\")\n        print(f\"Duration: {duration}s, Heater Power: {heater_power}%\")\n\n        # Reset simulator\n        initial_temp = self.simulator.reset()\n        print(f\"Initial temperature: {initial_temp:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Run open-loop test\n        results = self.simulator.run_open_loop(heater_power, duration)\n\n        # Format data for output\n        data = []\n        for i, result in enumerate(results):\n            data.append({\n                \"time\": result[\"time\"],\n                \"temperature\": result[\"temperature\"],\n                \"heater_power\": result[\"heater_power\"]\n            })\n\n        # Save calibration log\n        calibration_log = {\n            \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n            \"heater_power_test\": heater_power,\n            \"initial_temperature\": round(initial_temp, 2),\n            \"duration_seconds\": duration,\n            \"data\": data\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/calibration_log.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(calibration_log, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Collected {len(data)} data points\")\n        print(f\"Final temperature: {data[-1]['temperature']:.2f}°C\")\n        print(f\"Calibration log saved to: /root/calibration_log.json\")\n\n        return calibration_log\n\n    def estimate_parameters(self, calibration_log):\n        \"\"\"Estimate system parameters K and tau from calibration data\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PARAMETER ESTIMATION ===\")\n\n        data = calibration_log[\"data\"]\n        times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n        temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n        heater_power = calibration_log[\"heater_power_test\"]\n\n        # Initial temperature and ambient\n        T_initial = temps[0]\n        T_ambient = self.simulator.get_ambient_temp()\n\n        # Define first-order response model\n        def first_order_response(t, K, tau):\n            T_ss = T_ambient + K * heater_power\n            return T_ambient + (T_ss - T_ambient) * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n        try:\n            # Fit curve to data\n            popt, pcov = curve_fit(\n                first_order_response,\n                times,\n                temps,\n                p0=[0.1, 20.0],\n                maxfev=10000\n            )\n            K, tau = popt\n\n            # Calculate R² goodness of fit\n            predicted = first_order_response(times, K, tau)\n            residuals = temps - predicted\n            ss_res = np.sum(residuals**2)\n            ss_tot = np.sum((temps - np.mean(temps))**2)\n            r_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\n            fitting_error = np.sqrt(np.mean(residuals**2))\n\n            print(f\"Estimated K (gain): {K:.4f} °C per 1% power\")\n            print(f\"Estimated tau (time constant): {tau:.2f} seconds\")\n            print(f\"R² (goodness of fit): {r_squared:.4f}\")\n            print(f\"RMS fitting error: {fitting_error:.4f}°C\")\n\n            # Save estimated parameters\n            params = {\n                \"K\": round(K, 4),\n                \"tau\": round(tau, 2),\n                \"r_squared\": round(r_squared, 4),\n                \"fitting_error\": round(fitting_error, 4)\n            }\n\n            with open(\"/root/estimated_params.json\", \"w\") as f:\n                json.dump(params, f, indent=2)\n\n            print(f\"Parameters saved to: /root/estimated_params.json\")\n\n            return params\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error during parameter estimation: {e}\")\n            raise\n\n    def tune_pid_gains(self, params):\n        \"\"\"Calculate PID gains using lambda tuning method\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PID GAIN TUNING ===\")\n\n        K = params[\"K\"]\n        tau = params[\"tau\"]\n\n        # Lambda tuning: choose lambda for desired closed-loop response\n        # For this application: balance between speed and overshoot\n        lambda_tc = 2.5 * tau  # Choose lambda = 2.5 * tau\n\n        # Lambda tuning formulas for first-order system\n        Kp = tau / (K * lambda_tc)\n        Ki = Kp / tau\n        Kd = 0.0  # Not needed for temperature control\n\n        print(f\"Lambda (desired closed-loop time constant): {lambda_tc:.2f}s\")\n        print(f\"Kp (proportional gain): {Kp:.4f}\")\n        print(f\"Ki (integral gain): {Ki:.6f}\")\n        print(f\"Kd (derivative gain): {Kd:.4f}\")\n\n        # Save tuned gains\n        gains = {\n            \"Kp\": round(Kp, 4),\n            \"Ki\": round(Ki, 6),\n            \"Kd\": round(Kd, 4),\n            \"lambda\": round(lambda_tc, 2)\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/tuned_gains.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(gains, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Gains saved to: /root/tuned_gains.json\")\n\n        return gains\n\n    def run_closed_loop_control(self, gains, duration=150.0):\n        \"\"\"Run closed-loop control to reach setpoint\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== CLOSED-LOOP CONTROL ===\")\n        print(f\"Target setpoint: {self.setpoint}°C\")\n        print(f\"Control duration: {duration}s\")\n\n        # Create controller\n        controller = TemperatureController(\n            Kp=gains[\"Kp\"],\n            Ki=gains[\"Ki\"],\n            Kd=gains[\"Kd\"],\n            ts=self.dt\n        )\n\n        # Reset simulator\n        initial_temp = self.simulator.reset()\n\n        # Control loop\n        data = []\n        start_time = time.time()\n\n        # Run for duration seconds\n        step_count = int(duration / self.dt)\n\n        for step in range(step_count):\n            # Get current temperature measurement\n            result = self.simulator.step(0.0)  # Get measurement at current power\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            elapsed_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n            # Calculate control action\n            control_output = controller.update(self.setpoint, current_temp)\n\n            # Apply control\n            result = self.simulator.step(control_output)\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            elapsed_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n            # Log data\n            error = self.setpoint - current_temp\n            data.append({\n                \"time\": round(elapsed_time, 2),\n                \"temperature\": round(current_temp, 2),\n                \"setpoint\": round(self.setpoint, 2),\n                \"heater_power\": round(control_output, 2),\n                \"error\": round(error, 2)\n            })\n\n            # Print progress\n            if step % 20 == 0:\n                print(f\"t={elapsed_time:.1f}s: T={current_temp:.2f}°C, error={error:.2f}°C, power={control_output:.0f}%\")\n\n            # Check safety limit\n            if result[\"safety_triggered\"]:\n                print(f\"Safety limit triggered at t={elapsed_time:.1f}s\")\n                break\n\n        print(f\"\\nControl completed: {len(data)} data points collected\")\n        print(f\"Final temperature: {data[-1]['temperature']:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Save control log\n        control_log = {\n            \"phase\": \"control\",\n            \"setpoint\": self.setpoint,\n            \"controller_type\": \"PID\",\n            \"duration_seconds\": duration,\n            \"data\": data\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/control_log.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(control_log, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Control log saved to: /root/control_log.json\")\n\n        return control_log\n\n    def calculate_metrics(self, control_log):\n        \"\"\"Calculate performance metrics from control data\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PERFORMANCE METRICS ===\")\n\n        data = control_log[\"data\"]\n        times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n        temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n        setpoint = control_log[\"setpoint\"]\n\n        # Rise time (10%-90%)\n        idx_10 = np.where(temps >= setpoint * 0.1)[0]\n        idx_90 = np.where(temps >= setpoint * 0.9)[0]\n        if len(idx_10) > 0 and len(idx_90) > 0:\n            rise_time = times[idx_90[0]] - times[idx_10[0]]\n        else:\n            rise_time = None\n\n        # Overshoot\n        max_temp = np.max(temps)\n        overshoot = ((max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint * 100) if setpoint > 0 else 0\n\n        # Settling time (time to enter ±0.5°C band and stay)\n        settling_band = 0.5\n        in_band = np.abs(temps - setpoint) <= settling_band\n        if np.all(in_band[-50:]):  # Check last 50 points\n            idx_settle = np.where(in_band)[0]\n            if len(idx_settle) > 0:\n                settling_time = times[idx_settle[0]]\n            else:\n                settling_time = None\n        else:\n            settling_time = None\n\n        # Steady-state error\n        final_temps = temps[-50:]\n        steady_state_error = np.mean(np.abs(final_temps - setpoint))\n\n        # Maximum temperature\n        max_measured_temp = float(np.max(temps))\n\n        # Print metrics\n        print(f\"Rise time (10%-90%): {rise_time:.1f}s\" if rise_time else \"Rise time: N/A\")\n        print(f\"Overshoot: {overshoot:.2f}%\")\n        print(f\"Settling time (±0.5°C): {settling_time:.1f}s\" if settling_time else \"Settling time: N/A (not settled)\")\n        print(f\"Steady-state error: {steady_state_error:.2f}°C\")\n        print(f\"Maximum temperature: {max_measured_temp:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Check specs\n        specs_met = {\n            \"steady_state_error\": steady_state_error < 0.5,\n            \"settling_time\": settling_time is not None and settling_time < 120,\n            \"overshoot\": overshoot < 10.0,\n            \"max_temp\": max_measured_temp < 30.0\n        }\n\n        print(f\"\\nSpecifications met:\")\n        print(f\"  Steady-state error < 0.5°C: {specs_met['steady_state_error']}\")\n        print(f\"  Settling time < 120s: {specs_met['settling_time']}\")\n        print(f\"  Overshoot < 10%: {specs_met['overshoot']}\")\n        print(f\"  Max temp < 30°C: {specs_met['max_temp']}\")\n\n        # Save metrics\n        metrics = {\n            \"rise_time\": rise_time,\n            \"overshoot\": round(overshoot, 2),\n            \"settling_time\": settling_time,\n            \"steady_state_error\": round(steady_state_error, 2),\n            \"max_temp\": round(max_measured_temp, 2)\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/metrics.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"\\nMetrics saved to: /root/metrics.json\")\n\n        return metrics\n\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Run the complete control experiment\"\"\"\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n    print(\"TEMPERATURE CONTROLLER IMPLEMENTATION\")\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n\n    experiment = ControlSystemExperiment()\n\n    # Calibration: Characterize room thermal dynamics\n2. Parameter estimation: Extract K and tau\n3. PID tuning: Calculate controller gains\n4. Closed-loop control: Maintain setpoint temperature\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\n\nclass TemperatureController:\n    \"\"\"PID controller for temperature regulation\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, ts=0.5):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.ts = ts\n        self.integral_error = 0\n        self.last_error = 0\n\n    def update(self, setpoint, temperature):\n        \"\"\"Calculate control output using PID\"\"\"\n        error = setpoint - temperature\n\n        # Proportional term\n        p_term = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term with anti-windup\n        self.integral_error += error * self.ts\n        self.integral_error = np.clip(self.integral_error, -100, 100)\n        i_term = self.Ki * self.integral_error\n\n        # Derivative term\n        d_term = self.Kd * (error - self.last_error) / self.ts\n\n        # Calculate output\n        output = p_term + i_term + d_term\n        output = np.clip(output, 0, 100)\n\n        self.last_error = error\n        return output\n\n\nclass ControlSystemExperiment:\n    \"\"\"Main experiment orchestration\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.simulator = HVACSimulator()\n        self.dt = self.simulator.get_dt()\n        self.setpoint = self.simulator.get_setpoint()\n\n    def run_calibration(self, heater_power=50.0, duration=30.0):\n        \"\"\"Run calibration test with constant heater power\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== CALIBRATION TEST ===\")\n        print(f\"Duration: {duration}s, Heater Power: {heater_power}%\")\n\n        # Reset simulator\n        initial_temp = self.simulator.reset()\n        print(f\"Initial temperature: {initial_temp:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Run open-loop test\n        results = self.simulator.run_open_loop(heater_power, duration)\n\n        # Format data for output\n        data = []\n        for i, result in enumerate(results):\n            data.append({\n                \"time\": result[\"time\"],\n                \"temperature\": result[\"temperature\"],\n                \"heater_power\": result[\"heater_power\"]\n            })\n\n        # Save calibration log\n        calibration_log = {\n            \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n            \"heater_power_test\": heater_power,\n            \"initial_temperature\": round(initial_temp, 2),\n            \"duration_seconds\": duration,\n            \"data\": data\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/calibration_log.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(calibration_log, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Collected {len(data)} data points\")\n        print(f\"Final temperature: {data[-1]['temperature']:.2f}°C\")\n        print(f\"Calibration log saved to: /root/calibration_log.json\")\n\n        return calibration_log\n\n    def estimate_parameters(self, calibration_log):\n        \"\"\"Estimate system parameters K and tau from calibration data\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PARAMETER ESTIMATION ===\")\n\n        data = calibration_log[\"data\"]\n        times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n        temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n        heater_power = calibration_log[\"heater_power_test\"]\n\n        # Initial temperature and ambient\n        T_initial = temps[0]\n        T_ambient = self.simulator.get_ambient_temp()\n\n        # Define first-order response model\n        def first_order_response(t, K, tau):\n            T_ss = T_ambient + K * heater_power\n            return T_ambient + (T_ss - T_ambient) * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n        try:\n            # Fit curve to data\n            popt, pcov = curve_fit(\n                first_order_response,\n                times,\n                temps,\n                p0=[0.1, 20.0],\n                maxfev=10000\n            )\n            K, tau = popt\n\n            # Calculate R² goodness of fit\n            predicted = first_order_response(times, K, tau)\n            residuals = temps - predicted\n            ss_res = np.sum(residuals**2)\n            ss_tot = np.sum((temps - np.mean(temps))**2)\n            r_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\n            fitting_error = np.sqrt(np.mean(residuals**2))\n\n            print(f\"Estimated K (gain): {K:.4f} °C per 1% power\")\n            print(f\"Estimated tau (time constant): {tau:.2f} seconds\")\n            print(f\"R² (goodness of fit): {r_squared:.4f}\")\n            print(f\"RMS fitting error: {fitting_error:.4f}°C\")\n\n            # Save estimated parameters\n            params = {\n                \"K\": round(K, 4),\n                \"tau\": round(tau, 2),\n                \"r_squared\": round(r_squared, 4),\n                \"fitting_error\": round(fitting_error, 4)\n            }\n\n            with open(\"/root/estimated_params.json\", \"w\") as f:\n                json.dump(params, f, indent=2)\n\n            print(f\"Parameters saved to: /root/estimated_params.json\")\n\n            return params\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error during parameter estimation: {e}\")\n            raise\n\n    def tune_pid_gains(self, params):\n        \"\"\"Calculate PID gains using lambda tuning method\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PID GAIN TUNING ===\")\n\n        K = params[\"K\"]\n        tau = params[\"tau\"]\n\n        # Lambda tuning: choose lambda for desired closed-loop response\n        # For this application: balance between speed and overshoot\n        # Use more aggressive tuning (smaller lambda for faster response)\n        lambda_tc = 1.5 * tau  # Choose lambda = 1.5 * tau for faster response\n\n        # Lambda tuning formulas for first-order system\n        Kp = tau / (K * lambda_tc)\n        Ki = Kp / tau\n        Kd = 0.0  # Not needed for temperature control\n\n        print(f\"Lambda (desired closed-loop time constant): {lambda_tc:.2f}s\")\n        print(f\"Kp (proportional gain): {Kp:.4f}\")\n        print(f\"Ki (integral gain): {Ki:.6f}\")\n        print(f\"Kd (derivative gain): {Kd:.4f}\")\n\n        # Save tuned gains\n        gains = {\n            \"Kp\": round(Kp, 4),\n            \"Ki\": round(Ki, 6),\n            \"Kd\": round(Kd, 4),\n            \"lambda\": round(lambda_tc, 2)\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/tuned_gains.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(gains, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Gains saved to: /root/tuned_gains.json\")\n\n        return gains\n\n    def run_closed_loop_control(self, gains, duration=150.0):\n        \"\"\"Run closed-loop control to reach setpoint\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== CLOSED-LOOP CONTROL ===\")\n        print(f\"Target setpoint: {self.setpoint}°C\")\n        print(f\"Control duration: {duration}s\")\n\n        # Create controller\n        controller = TemperatureController(\n            Kp=gains[\"Kp\"],\n            Ki=gains[\"Ki\"],\n            Kd=gains[\"Kd\"],\n            ts=self.dt\n        )\n\n        # Reset simulator\n        initial_temp = self.simulator.reset()\n\n        # Control loop\n        data = []\n        start_time = time.time()\n\n        # Run for duration seconds\n        step_count = int(duration / self.dt)\n\n        for step in range(step_count):\n            # Get current temperature measurement\n            result = self.simulator.step(0.0)  # Get measurement at current power\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            elapsed_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n            # Calculate control action\n            control_output = controller.update(self.setpoint, current_temp)\n\n            # Apply control\n            result = self.simulator.step(control_output)\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            elapsed_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n            # Log data\n            error = self.setpoint - current_temp\n            data.append({\n                \"time\": round(elapsed_time, 2),\n                \"temperature\": round(current_temp, 2),\n                \"setpoint\": round(self.setpoint, 2),\n                \"heater_power\": round(control_output, 2),\n                \"error\": round(error, 2)\n            })\n\n            # Print progress\n            if step % 20 == 0:\n                print(f\"t={elapsed_time:.1f}s: T={current_temp:.2f}°C, error={error:.2f}°C, power={control_output:.0f}%\")\n\n            # Check safety limit\n            if result[\"safety_triggered\"]:\n                print(f\"Safety limit triggered at t={elapsed_time:.1f}s\")\n                break\n\n        print(f\"\\nControl completed: {len(data)} data points collected\")\n        print(f\"Final temperature: {data[-1]['temperature']:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Save control log\n        control_log = {\n            \"phase\": \"control\",\n            \"setpoint\": self.setpoint,\n            \"controller_type\": \"PID\",\n            \"duration_seconds\": duration,\n            \"data\": data\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/control_log.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(control_log, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"Control log saved to: /root/control_log.json\")\n\n        return control_log\n\n    def calculate_metrics(self, control_log):\n        \"\"\"Calculate performance metrics from control data\"\"\"\n        print(f\"\\n=== PERFORMANCE METRICS ===\")\n\n        data = control_log[\"data\"]\n        times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n        temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n        setpoint = control_log[\"setpoint\"]\n\n        # Rise time (10%-90%)\n        idx_10 = np.where(temps >= setpoint * 0.1)[0]\n        idx_90 = np.where(temps >= setpoint * 0.9)[0]\n        if len(idx_10) > 0 and len(idx_90) > 0:\n            rise_time = times[idx_90[0]] - times[idx_10[0]]\n        else:\n            rise_time = None\n\n        # Overshoot\n        max_temp = np.max(temps)\n        overshoot = ((max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint * 100) if setpoint > 0 else 0\n\n        # Settling time (time to enter ±0.5°C band and stay)\n        settling_band = 0.5\n        in_band = np.abs(temps - setpoint) <= settling_band\n        if np.all(in_band[-50:]):  # Check last 50 points\n            idx_settle = np.where(in_band)[0]\n            if len(idx_settle) > 0:\n                settling_time = times[idx_settle[0]]\n            else:\n                settling_time = None\n        else:\n            settling_time = None\n\n        # Steady-state error\n        final_temps = temps[-50:]\n        steady_state_error = np.mean(np.abs(final_temps - setpoint))\n\n        # Maximum temperature\n        max_measured_temp = float(np.max(temps))\n\n        # Print metrics\n        print(f\"Rise time (10%-90%): {rise_time:.1f}s\" if rise_time else \"Rise time: N/A\")\n        print(f\"Overshoot: {overshoot:.2f}%\")\n        print(f\"Settling time (±0.5°C): {settling_time:.1f}s\" if settling_time else \"Settling time: N/A (not settled)\")\n        print(f\"Steady-state error: {steady_state_error:.2f}°C\")\n        print(f\"Maximum temperature: {max_measured_temp:.2f}°C\")\n\n        # Check specs\n        specs_met = {\n            \"steady_state_error\": steady_state_error < 0.5,\n            \"settling_time\": settling_time is not None and settling_time < 120,\n            \"overshoot\": overshoot < 10.0,\n            \"max_temp\": max_measured_temp < 30.0\n        }\n\n        print(f\"\\nSpecifications met:\")\n        print(f\"  Steady-state error < 0.5°C: {specs_met['steady_state_error']}\")\n        print(f\"  Settling time < 120s: {specs_met['settling_time']}\")\n        print(f\"  Overshoot < 10%: {specs_met['overshoot']}\")\n        print(f\"  Max temp < 30°C: {specs_met['max_temp']}\")\n\n        # Save metrics\n        metrics = {\n            \"rise_time\": rise_time,\n            \"overshoot\": round(overshoot, 2),\n            \"settling_time\": settling_time,\n            \"steady_state_error\": round(steady_state_error, 2),\n            \"max_temp\": round(max_measured_temp, 2)\n        }\n\n        with open(\"/root/metrics.json\", \"w\") as f:\n            json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2)\n\n        print(f\"\\nMetrics saved to: /root/metrics.json\")\n\n        return metrics\n\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Run the complete control experiment\"\"\"\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n    print(\"TEMPERATURE CONTROLLER IMPLEMENTATION\")\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n\n    experiment = ControlSystemExperiment()\n\n    #
